Nits: `ManagerVerdict`'s docstring, `managers.py` naming `manager_prompt()`, one `LinkRecords.head_of()` (the newest pull link's head) for both readers, `_standing` on one of the manager's own projects, `ManagerVerdict` in `readiness()`/`_blockers()`, and the note on `refused_for()`'s per-request read. **Review round 2 (fixed test-first):** `plan`'s scope now covers a task nobody is on — editing or splitting one is held to the features its reports work on, and `_plans_in()` counts the whole subtree rather than direct reports, as every other power does; a person's task is refused by name (a person plans their own, 28.2), and a feature whose only work is a person's is not one a manager plans in. `_managing()` writes `Agent.last_news` only once the runner has said something: `_work()` returns whether the turn reached the harness, so a runner that refuses to start leaves the news untold and the next tick says it again (a manager stuck otherwise). Tests: 4 in `test_managers.py` (an unassigned task edited and split in its reports' feature and refused elsewhere, a person's task refused, a person's-only feature refused) and `test_a_turn_that_never_started_does_not_count_as_told` in `test_dispatcher.py`. **Review round 3:** the news counts as told only when the turn ended done — a yielded `RunnerLimited` or `RunnerFailed` said something but carried none of the news, so `_work()` returns `outcome is Outcome.DONE` and a limited manager hears it again once the limit lifts (`test_a_limited_turn_does_not_count_the_news_as_told`). **Follow-up, the `_may_size` hole (PR #73, after 26.8's merge):** `Orch._may_size()` let a manager with `plan` size any task; it now holds the power to the manager's own subtree itself — `_in_subtree()` for a task someone is on, `_plans_in()` for one nobody is on — instead of relying on the edit that carries the size, which already checks the task (the second walk of the tree is what that costs, on a rare edit, and `_may_size()` is the backstop). Tested on the power itself: 2 unit in `test_sizes.py` call `_may_size()` directly and fail on `main` (so the rule is pinned to the operation, not to `set_task`), and 1 integration in `test_managers.py` walks it end to end.

**27.9 Operations** · needs 27.1–27.8
- Build: besides those named above: `orch_list_agents`, `orch_get_agent` (with its queue, session and reports), `orch_set_agent` (admin), `orch_delete_agent` (admin; `InUse` while it has reports or an unfinished task), `orch_pause_agent` / `orch_resume_agent`, `orch_stop_session`, `orch_get_session` (the transcript, paged), `orch_send` (owner only: a message typed into an agent's session), and 25.11's runner operations joined to `orch`'s registry. CLI under `udl orch agents …`, `udl orch queue …`, `udl orch suggest`. `GET /orch/sessions/{id}/stream` (SSE of session events) is interface-only.
- Tests first: an interface test per operation and its `MATRIX` row.
- Done when: the parity test lists them all.
- **Built (deepseek-b, 22 Nov 2026, PR #75):** the nine operations — `orch_list_agents`, `orch_get_agent` (its status, queue, session and reports), `orch_set_agent` (admin; a new one needs a title and a runner, `manager: ""` clears it), `orch_delete_agent` (admin; `InUse` while it has reports or a queued/in-progress task — the task half is new in `AgentRecords.delete_agent`), `orch_pause_agent` / `orch_resume_agent`, `orch_get_session` (the transcript, paged), `orch_stop_session` (its runner is told, and it is ended so nothing more is dispatched), and `orch_send` (owner only). The shapes live in `orch/agents.py` (the models' own base) rather than `models.py`, because `models` and `agents` import each other; `as_shape`'s bound is now `BaseModel` so an agent shape can be built from its record, and `agents.an_event` is the one place a runner event becomes a transcript event (the dispatcher's `_an_event` calls it). `GET /orch/sessions/{session}/stream` is interface-only, as the card says (listed in `API_ONLY`). CLI: `udl orch agents [list|show|set|rm|pause|resume|session|stop|send]`, `udl orch queue [show|mine|add|set|rm]`, `udl orch suggest`; `queue`'s read is `queue show` because a group's own positional argument is not run by Typer. Tests: 19 in `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_agent_operations.py`, the `MATRIX` rows in `tests/interface/test_orch_operations.py` with three run for real (agents over the CLI, a queue over the CLI, `orch_suggest` over MCP), and `orch_session_stream` in `API_ONLY`. One turn per session, across processes (review round 3): `Turns.one` holds the session's in-process lock **and** a lease, `orch-turn:<session id>`, the way 27.5's gate lane is `orch-gate:<host>` — so a person's typed turn in the CLI's process and the loop's in `udl serve` never run one session at once. `orch_send` claims it without waiting (refused "is in a turn" while another holder has it), the loop's turn waits for it up to `TURN_WAIT` (10 min, and a turn that never gets it is no turn: the session is left as it was — not counted, not failed, not ended) — and what the prompt said it told (an answer, the pull's news) is written once its turn has *run*, not when the prompt is built — so a turn that never starts told nothing, and the write goes onto the session as it is stored then, never the copy taken before the turn (its turn number, its tokens and cost, and the state it ended in stay the turn's, and an `ENDED` a person gave it mid-turn stays); a `Wait` closes at the time the turn began, not when it ended, the lease is renewed while the turn runs, and an expired one is taken over, so a process that died mid-turn still lets the restart in. Its holder is `<host>:<pid>:<8 hex>`, unique per `Turns`, so two `Turns` of one process are two holders as two processes' are. Tests: `test_a_turn_waits_for_the_other_process_s_turn`, `test_a_message_is_refused_while_another_process_turns_the_session`, `test_a_turn_whose_process_died_is_taken_over`, `test_a_long_turn_keeps_its_lease`, `test_a_turn_that_is_cancelled_ends_and_gives_its_lease_back`, `test_a_turn_that_never_gets_its_lease_is_no_turn`, and the loop's own two paths in `test_a_session_another_process_is_turning_is_left_alone` (its answer still to come) and `test_a_manager_s_session_another_process_is_turning_is_left_alone` (its news not counted as told), `test_a_turn_that_says_what_changed_keeps_the_turns_it_ran` (the turn's number, its tokens and the `ENDED` it gave the session survive) and `test_a_session_a_person_stops_mid_turn_stays_ended` (a stop mid-turn is not overwritten) — both fail on the copy-saved version. `test_an_answer_reaches_the_agent_s_next_session` no longer counts turns: it lowers `max_nudges` to 2 and waits for the stall's question (`until_there`), because how many turns one tick runs is the dispatcher's to decide, not a test's to assume.
- **Review round (deepseek-b, 22 Nov 2026, PR #75):** twelve items, all fixed. (1) **`orch_send` was a second turn loop:** it is now the dispatcher's own — `dispatcher.Turns.one`, the numbering (`session.turns`), `RUNNING` before the turn and the session's turn lock, its `orch.session.event`s, `Usage` into the cost, and `_ended`'s meaning of a limit or a failure; a turn it cannot start (the session is running, or a person ended it) answers `NotAllowed` rather than pretending. (2) **A stop mid-turn was undone:** every save a turn makes now reloads the session and leaves an `ENDED` one alone (`Turns._saved`), `Outcome.STOPPED` ends the loop's turn without retrying, and a stop the harness refuses writes why into `last_error` instead of passing. (3) **Owner controls open to agents:** `orch_pause_agent`, `orch_resume_agent`, `orch_stop_session` and `orch_get_session` are `admin=True`, which a reviewer's role table refuses (four parametrised tests). (4) **`steer`'s docstrings** now say it is refused, and why, and `Sent.turn` is an `int`. (5) **A queue of every project's tasks said no project:** `QueueEntry.project` says which (a test with two `F-1.1`s). (6) **The stream had no filter test, and its description promised a quota event it drops:** `tests/interface/api/test_orch_session_stream.py` publishes another session's event and an `orch.agent.limited`, and only the session's own arrives; the description says a limit is the agent's type. (7) **No `Any`** in the tests' signatures, and the missing tests are there: an agent refused `orch_send`, `orch_list_agents` by project, paging from an offset, a runner that raises on stop, a stop mid-turn, the four owner controls. (8) `_AGENTS`/`_QUEUE` moved above `_RUNNERS`' docstring. (9) The dead `_AN_EVENT` and `_an_event` wrapper are gone. (10) The transcript read stays one read, with why in `SessionRecords.transcript`'s docstring: it is one session's own events (bounded by its turns), and a manager-level count cannot sum across databases without counting a mirrored record twice. (11) **"An interface test per operation":** twelve of the fifteen now run for real over the CLI (a parametrised sweep), and the three a CLI cannot set up (a session's read, its stop, a typed message) over the API. (12) **Tests first, in one commit:** the repo's pre-commit hook runs `make check` on every commit, so a commit whose test names a field the fix adds (`QueueEntry.project`) fails pyright and is refused; the tests were written and seen failing before the fix in this round, and the two go in one commit.
- **Follow-up round (deepseek-b, 22 Nov 2026, PR #75):** three, and the transcript item fixed after all. **A turn could bring an `ENDED` session back to `RUNNING`:** `Turns._one_turn` read the session again *under* the lock (it read it before, outside), so a session a turn or a stop ended while this one waited stays ended and this turn does not start — and the number a turn runs as comes from that read, not the caller's snapshot (`Ran.turn`; `Sent.turn` is it). Two tests: a message that waits on the lock while the session is ended is refused and leaves it ended, and one that waits while a turn lands numbers its own turn. **The lock table grew for ever:** `_LOCKS` is a `WeakValueDictionary`, so a turn holds its lock while it runs and a session nobody is turning leaves none behind (a test). **The transcript, after all:** `orch_list_agents` no longer parses one — `AgentInfo.session` is the session's own shape (`AgentSessionShape`), not `SessionInfo` with a transcript count, and a test makes `SessionRecords.transcript` raise to prove the list never reads it. `orch_get_session` still reads one session's transcript, which is what it is for. The `_AGENTS` and `_QUEUE` CLI words have docstrings and the same `tuple[str, ...]` type as `_RUNNERS`.
- **Left out, and why:** `delivery: "steer"` is refused with a reason (`a running turn takes no input yet`): none of udl's harnesses takes a message mid-turn, and pretending to steer would put words in the transcript that the runner never saw — 27.11's chats need the same answer for their permission prompts. "25.11's runner operations joined to `orch`'s registry" is not done: they are already offered as `udl runners …` on every interface, and joining them means renaming each one and moving its `MATRIX` row, which is a naming decision (Claude's) rather than a build step.

**27.10 Working time, learned hours and forecasts** · needs 26.8, 27.7 · size XL (O-D14, O-C14)
- Build:
  - **Recording time.** Two kinds of record:
    - `StateChange` (scope: history): `task`, `from`, `to`, `at`, `by`. It is written in the same write as every move (26.3's `move()`, and `save_all`), so a task's history is complete.
    - `Wait`: `task`, `kind: ci | bot | limit`, `start`, `end`. The dispatcher (27.7) writes it while the agent holds no turn because its PR's checks or the review bot are pending, and while the agent is `limited`.
  - **`timing.py`** (pure): `working_time(changes, waits) -> timedelta`.
    - It is the time spent `in_progress`, less the waits inside it; a wait that crosses a state change counts only for the part inside `in_progress`.
    - A task that moved between agents counts each agent's part for that agent's model.
  - **`estimates.py`** (pure):
    - `hours_per_point(samples) -> Rate` takes merged, sized tasks: the median of working hours ÷ points, per model (the model of the agent that worked the task), over its last 30.
    - A model needs at least 5 samples; below that the project's rate is used, then a default of 1 h a point. `Rate` says which it used (`model`, `project`, `default`).
    - `estimate(task, agent) -> timedelta` is the size × that rate.
    - The spread is the 25th and 75th percentile of actual ÷ estimate, over the same samples.
  - **`forecast(project, now) -> Forecast`** (pure):
    - It takes the open, sized work per agent in queue order: what is left of its current task's estimate, then each queued task's estimate.
    - Unassigned tasks are spread over the project's enabled implementers in suggestion order (27.4).
    - An agent's hours become days at its own working hours per day over the last 7 days. Below 1 h a day (a new or idle agent), the project's agents' average is used, then 8 h.
    - `likely` is when the last agent finishes; `early` and `late` come from the spread. The same is done per feature.
    - Unsized tasks are left out, and counted.
  - **`orch_progress`** (read-only; `project`, `since?`) → `Progress`:
    - `series`: for each day, the scope's points and the points merged. A day's scope counts the tasks that existed and weren't cancelled then, at their size now.
    - `pace`: points merged a day, over the last 7 days.
    - `forecast`, and `features` (key, title, points, merged, forecast).
    - `models`: model, runner, tasks, hours per point, and hours per size.
    - `recent`: the last 20 merged, sized tasks (key, title, size, estimate, actual), and `estimate_ratio`, the median of actual ÷ estimate.
  - **`orch_estimate`** (`task`): one task's size, estimate, and the time worked on it so far.
- Tests first: `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_timing.py` and `test_estimates.py`, on `FakeClock` histories:
  - waits taken off, including one across a state change, and a moved task split between two models;
  - a model with fewer than five samples falls back, and says so;
  - the forecast for two agents whose rates are known;
  - an agent with no working hours in the last 7 days, and a project with none at all, still forecast (no division by zero);
  - a property test: a forecast never ends before `now`, and `early ≤ likely ≤ late`.

  `test_progress_operations.py` goes through the service.
- Done when: `udl orch progress udl` prints the burn-up's numbers, the forecast and the hours per point, on a fixture history.
- **Built (`deepseek-a`, 21 Nov 2026, PR to follow):** the two records — `StateChange` (a task's
  move, written in the same write as the move itself by `TaskRecords.save_all`, so a task's
  history is complete) and `Wait` (`ci`, `bot` or `limit`, one open at a time per task, opened by
  the dispatcher where it already knew the agent held no turn: its pull's checks or the bot
  pending, or its harness out of quota, and closed when a turn starts). (`from_state`/`to_state`
  and `wait_kind`: `from` is a keyword and `kind` is a record's own name.) `timing.py` is
  `working_time` and `by_agent` (a task handed on is split between the agents that worked it),
  with a `since` so a week's hours are a week's; `estimates.py` is the median of a model's hours
  a point over its last 30 merged sized tasks, the project's rate below five samples, then an
  hour a point — and the `Rate` says which — plus `estimate(points, rate)` and `spread` (the
  25th/75th of actual ÷ estimated, which is what makes a forecast a range). `progress.py` is the
  series, the pace and `finishes` (an agent's hours at its own day, the project's average below
  1 h a day, then 8 h; never before now). `reporting.py` reads the records into them, and
  `orch_progress`/`orch_estimate` (read-only, `udl orch progress <project>` and
  `udl orch estimate <project> <task>`) answer `Progress` and `Estimate`. Unassigned work is
  spread over the enabled implementers in name order (27.4's suggestion order is not used yet).
  Tests: `test_timing.py` (12), `test_estimates.py` (9), `test_forecast.py` (10),
  `test_history.py` (7), `test_progress_operations.py` (11, through the service on a fixture
  history), the CLI's `udl orch progress udl` (the human output and its `--json`), and the
  dispatcher's checks and quota tests now assert their `Wait`. `Readiness.pending_kinds`
  (`checks`, `mergeable`, `bot`) is the typed reason the dispatcher's wait kind is read from;
  a manager who has not reviewed the head counts as a `bot` one, so `orch_pr_status --wait` waits
  on the manager as it does on the bot. Only a feature's merged points read an imported `[x]` as a
  merge (an import writes no move, so its hours are unknown and it is in no series, recent list
  or rate). The merge path and the plugin's own moves write their `StateChange`s on the app's
  clock.

**27.11 Chat sessions** · needs 27.2, 27.9, 25.8, and 25.12 for its Codex half only (25.12 is optional, Q-9: the Claude Code half doesn't wait for it) · size XL (O-D15, O-C15)
- Build: `orch/chat.py`, the sessions behind the chat sidebar (29.12).
  - **`Chat`**: `id`, `owner` (`human:<name>`), `runner` (`claude-code` or `codex`), `model`, `host`, `directory`, `ref: SessionRef`, `state` (an `AgentSession`'s states), `title` (from the first message), `created_at`, `last_at`, `tokens_in`, `tokens_out`, `cost`.
    - `directory` is where the harness starts: any directory on that host the user picks, by default the host's udl checkout.
    - A chat's stream is `SessionEvent`s, as an agent session's is (27.1), so the live view and the transcript are shared code.
  - **As the user** (O-C15). A chat's turn runs the runner with the chat's own options, not an agent's:
    - none of `--settings` with a deny file, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--permission-mode` or a worktree: the user's own settings decide;
    - udl's MCP server is added beside the user's own;
    - never `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, `--permission-mode bypassPermissions` or Codex's bypass flag (asserted).
  - **Permission prompts reach the sidebar.**
    - **`claude-code`:** `--permission-prompt-tool mcp__udl__orch_chat_ask_permission`, an MCP-only tool (not on the CLI or the API). The harness calls it, with the tool and its input, whenever the user's settings say to ask.
    - udl then records a `PermissionRequest` (`chat`, `id`, `tool`, `input` kept as data, `state`) and publishes `orch.chat.permission`. It waits up to 10 min, then denies, for `orch_chat_permission` (`chat`, `request`, `decision: allow_once | allow_chat | deny`, `message?`).
    - `allow_chat` remembers that exact request (the tool and its exact input: the whole command, or the same file) for the rest of the chat, and answers it itself when it comes again. Nothing is matched by prefix. A shell command that chains or nests others (`&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, `$(…)`, backticks), or runs a shell or `env`, is never remembered, so Allow for this chat on one is Allow once.
    - **`codex`:** drive `codex app-server`, the JSON-RPC protocol over stdio that OpenAI's IDE extension uses. Its approval requests become the same `PermissionRequest`s. Read its methods from the installed version (`codex app-server generate-json-schema --out <dir>`, committed as fixtures).
    - Without `app-server`, a Codex chat isn't started: `orch_chat_start` refuses it (`NotAllowed`: "this Codex can't relay its approvals; update it"), since running without approvals would break O-C15. A Codex agent (25.12) is another matter: it works in a worktree, under its command rules.
  - **The chat's token** (O-C15): a `ChatToken`.
    - It has the `udl_chat_` prefix. The record keeps its SHA-256; the token itself is in the secret store at `apps/orch/chats/<id>/token`, and reaches the harness in its environment.
    - 27.2's `authenticate` reads it as `Principal(kind="owner", name=<owner>, via=f"chat:{id}")`.
    - `UDLService.call` refuses it `orch_issue_token`, `orch_set_auto_merge` (28.7), the secret store's writes, and every `orch_chat_*` operation but one: `orch_chat_ask_permission`, the prompt tool its harness calls. For that one, the chat is the token's own (taken from its `via`, never from the request), and `orch_chat_permission` stays refused, so a chat can't answer its own prompts.
    - It is deleted when the chat is stopped or deleted, or has been idle for `[apps.orch] chat_idle` (default 24 h). Every call made with it is logged with its `via`.
  - **Operations,** owner only (an agent's token is refused, `NotAllowed`):
    - `orch_chat_start` (`runner`, `host`, `directory?`, `model?`, `text`);
    - `orch_chat_send` (`chat`, `text`: queued while a turn runs, or steered where the runner can);
    - `orch_chat_permission`;
    - `orch_chat_stop`, which ends the turn;
    - `orch_chat_list` (the user's chats, newest first) and `orch_chat_get` (the transcript, paged);
    - `orch_chat_delete`.

    `GET /orch/chats/{id}/stream` (an SSE of its events and permission requests) is interface-only, as 27.9's session stream is. The CLI has `udl orch chat`: an interactive loop over the same operations, with prompts answered in the terminal.
  - **Text from outside:** what a chat reads (PR comments, bot reviews, pages) is its harness's business, as it is in a terminal. udl's own messages to it never carry text from outside.
- Tests first: `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_chat.py`, with `FakeRunner` and a fake `claude` that asks for one permission:
  - the request reaches `orch.chat.permission`;
  - Allow once runs it, Deny stops it, Allow for this chat answers the same request by itself the second time but asks again for `git push` after `git status` and for `ls && rm x` after `ls`, and no answer in 10 min denies;
  - the argv never has a flag that skips permissions;
  - the chat's token is refused `orch_issue_token`, `orch_chat_start` and `orch_chat_permission`, can call `orch_chat_ask_permission` for its own chat only, works for `orch_assign`, and stops working after `orch_chat_stop`;
  - an agent's token can't start a chat.
- Done when: an opt-in smoke test on `local`, with the real `claude`, starts a chat that asks to run `ls`; it is allowed through `orch_chat_permission`, and its answer names a file.

- **Built (`deepseek-b`, 24 Nov 2026, PR #96):** the Claude Code half, whole. **The record** is `orch_chat` (`Chat`: `owner`, `runner`, `model`, `permission_mode`, `host`, `directory`, `ref`, `state`, `title`, `turns`, `tokens_in`/`tokens_out`, `cost`, `last_error`, `last_text`, and `pending` — what a person said while a turn ran, each the next turn, since a chat takes one at a time); its transcript is the `SessionEvent`s an agent's session keeps (27.1), so the live view and the transcript are the same code. The card's `last_at` is the record's `updated_at`. **A chat's token** is `orch_chat_token` (`ChatToken`: its SHA-256, never the token, which is in the secret store at `apps/orch/chats/<id>/token`), one per chat, issued at start and rotated by issuing again; `udl.interfaces.security`'s `Principal` grew `PrincipalKind.CHAT` and its `chat`, which is the card's `via` as a field — `OrchTokens.principal_for` answers it as the person the chat is, and the chat it is for. **What it may not do** is the general rule in `tokens.refused`: every `orch_chat_*` operation but `orch_chat_ask_permission` (whose chat is taken from the principal, never the request, so a chat cannot answer its own prompts or reach another's), `orch_issue_token`, `orch_set_auto_merge` (28.7's switch refuses anything that is not the owner, so a chat's token is refused there too, with a test for each of the gate and the operation), the secret store's writes, and — because a chat has no business changing an account, an agent or a setting — every admin operation. **`chat_idle`** (`[apps.orch]`, 24 h) is `OrchTokens`': a chat's token whose chat was untouched for longer is refused and deleted as it is refused (the book asks the app for its settings once, and a broken app's settings never stop authentication); every call made with a token is logged with the chat it is for, and the secret goes with it (the file its harness read it from is taken out of the folder when the chat stops or is deleted: `_forget_files`). **As the user:** `SessionSpec.own_settings` is the chat's own arguments, passed to the harness as given (a test says so), and no `--settings`, `--strict-mcp-config`, `--permission-mode`, worktree or bypass flag is ever added. **Permission prompts** reach the person, in Claude Code's own protocol: the harness calls the tool with `tool_name`, `input` and `tool_use_id` (never a chat — the token's own is the one it reaches) and reads back `{behavior: allow, updatedInput}` or `{behavior: deny, message}`; udl records the `ChatPermission` (`orch.chat.permission`), waits up to `chat_permission_minutes` (a quarter-second between reads, not twenty a second), then denies; `orch_chat_permission` answers `allow_once | allow_chat | deny`; `allow_chat` remembers that exact tool and input for the rest of the chat and answers it itself, and a shell command that chains, nests or runs a shell — `&&`, `||`, `;`, `|`, a lone `&`, a newline, `$(`, backticks, `<(`/`>(`, `sh`/`bash`/`env`/`xargs`/`sudo`/`eval`/`exec`/`nohup`/`timeout`/`ssh`/…, or a leading `VAR=x` — is never remembered (Allow for this chat is Allow once for it). **Operations:** `orch_chat_start`, `orch_chat_send` (queued while a turn runs), `orch_chat_permission`, `orch_chat_stop` (which deletes the token), `orch_chat_list`, `orch_chat_get` (paged) and `orch_chat_delete`, with `GET /orch/chats/{id}/stream` interface-only as 27.9's session stream is. **The CLI loop** is `udl orch chat`: the one chat command that is not an operation — `cli_groups(..., written=…)` mounts it in the `orch` group, and it prints what the harness says as it says it — it looks at the chat every half second while the person types, so a prompt shows when it is asked, not when the next line is typed — asks the person what it asks, and sends what they type (a test per decision, and the usage rules). **Tests, written first:** 18 in `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_chat.py` (start and its transcript, a message as a turn, the permission asked and answered each way, `allow_chat` remembered and a chained shell command not, a prompt nobody answers in ten minutes denied with `FakeClock`, a chat left alone for a day ended with its token and secret gone, an agent's token refused `orch_chat_start` and a chat's refused an account's and an agent's settings, the owner refused the harness's prompt tool, udl's files taken out of the folder when a chat stops, the token's refusals, stop deleting the token, a person's stop staying stopped, `last_text`), 3 in `test_tokens.py` (a chat's token is its chat while used; untouched for a day, refused and taken away; gone with its chat), 2 in `test_auto_merge.py` (the gate and the operation's own check), 37 in `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_chat_rules.py` (every shell command that is never remembered, a redirect included), 35 in `tests/unit/interfaces/cli/test_chats.py` (the loop's rules and decisions — a word alone decides, so "actually, don't" and "chat about it first" deny — and the loop itself: what is typed is a turn, a prompt answered elsewhere is not answered twice, a prompt is shown once, a refused second answer goes as a message, and a line that plainly answers a closed prompt is not sent as a turn). `openapi.json` and the typed client regenerated. **Left out:** the card's Codex half (`codex app-server` and its approval requests, and `orch_chat_start` refusing a Codex that cannot relay them) is Q-9's: it needs 25.12, which is not built, so it waits for it. The opt-in smoke test with the real `claude` is the user's to run on `local` (it costs money and needs their harness signed in). **Deviations, recorded:** `orch_chat_ask_permission` is on the API as well as MCP, because udl's parity rule is one operation set for every interface; what keeps it safe is that only a chat's token may call it (the owner is refused), and the chat is the token's own. `orch_chat_list` counts each chat's transcript to answer its total: the database kinds have no count query (`records.py`'s `transcript` says why), so a list of many long chats reads more than a list of ids would — the follow-up is a count in the database kinds, which belongs with 31's data layer.

**27.12 Resource limits: gates and sessions under a cap** · needs 25.6, 27.5, 27.7 · size M (the user, 23 Nov 2026)
- Why: on 22 Nov one agent's runaway test grew to 22.9 GiB, the kernel's OOM killer took it, and systemd stopped the scope the harness ran in, so every agent was down for 90 minutes. Claude's supervision has run every test under `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=4G -p MemorySwapMax=0 timeout …` since. This makes it udl's.
- Build:
  - **Settings** (`OrchSettings`, `[apps.orch]`):
    - `gates_per_host: PositiveInt = 1`. The user chose a setting defaulting to 1, as 27.5 built it: a bigger host can raise it.
    - `gate_memory: ByteSize | None = "4GiB"` and `session_memory: ByteSize | None = "20GiB"` (None: no cap).
    - `Repo` gains `gate_cleanup: Gate | None = None`: an argv run after a gate that was stopped (see below).
  - **`GateLane` with N slots:** `gates_per_host` leases per host, `orch-gate:<host>:<n>` for `n` in `0…N-1`. A push takes the first free one and waits as today when all are held. With N = 1 it behaves exactly as now.
  - **A capped run (`HostShell`):** `run()` takes `limits: ResourceLimits | None` (`memory: ByteSize`, swap always 0) and `unit: str`.
    - **Where systemd is there:** a host where `systemd-run --user --scope -q true` succeeds (probed once per host and kept until the host changes) runs the argv as `systemd-run --user --scope -q --unit=<unit> -p MemoryMax=<bytes> -p MemorySwapMax=0 -- <argv>`.
    - **Elsewhere** (no user systemd, macOS), it runs as today, and the result says `limits="unenforced"`, never silently.
  - **Gates:** `push()` runs the repo's `gate` with `gate_memory`, as unit `udl-gate-<project>-<key>-<8 hex>`.
    - **Unit names:** each part (`<project>`, `<key>`) goes through `systemd-escape`, so a key like `27.3a` or a project with a slash makes a valid name. The unit is a scope, so it is always named with its suffix, `<unit>.scope`: a bare name means `.service` to `systemctl`.
    - **The cap is hit:** a capped run that ends on `SIGKILL` (exit 137, or `-9` from the shell) when udl didn't stop it was stopped by the kernel's OOM killer at the cap. `PushResult` gets `stopped = "memory"` and the message "the gate passed its memory cap of 4 GiB", and nothing is pushed. (The scope's `memory.events` and `Result` are gone once a transient scope ends, so they aren't read.)
    - **On a timeout** (27.5's `gate_minutes`), udl stops the unit itself with `systemctl --user stop <unit>.scope`, so its children go too, and `stopped = "timeout"`. udl records that it stopped it, so this is never read as `memory`.
    - **After either,** the repo's `gate_cleanup` runs in the worktree (5 minutes at most, its tail added to the result). For udl itself it's unset, since `tests/support/servers.py` reaps its own containers (#74).
  - **Sessions:** a runner that starts a process per turn through `HostShell` (`claude-code`, `codex`, `command`) runs it with `session_memory`, as unit `udl-session-<agent>-<8 hex>`, so a runaway in a turn is that turn's failure (`RunnerFailed`, "the session passed its memory cap"), not the host's.
    - `opencode` runs as a service: 25.11's harness status reports the service's `MemoryMax` and `OOMPolicy` and warns when there's no cap, or when `OOMPolicy` isn't `continue` (an OOM kill would otherwise stop the whole service). udl doesn't edit units.
- Tests first:
  - with a `FakeShell` recording argv: wrapped when the probe succeeds, plain with `unenforced` when it fails;
  - the stop argv names `<unit>.scope`, and a key such as `27.3a` makes an escaped, valid unit name;
  - a capped run ending on `SIGKILL` that udl didn't stop reads `memory`, and one udl stopped reads `timeout`;
  - `gates_per_host = 2` lets two pushes gate at once and a third waits;
  - a gate stopped by its cap pushes nothing and says `memory`;
  - a timeout stops the unit and says `timeout`;
  - `gate_cleanup` runs only after a stop;
  - a session's turn is wrapped, and its cap is `RunnerFailed`.
- Done when:
  - on the fakes, all of the above;
  - on jeff-wiggle, an opt-in `-m host` test whose gate allocates past a 256 MiB cap is stopped and reported `memory` while the machine stays up. Claude runs it.

**27.13 Host health, and what udl does about it** · needs 27.12, 28.2, 25.11 · size L (the user, 23 Nov 2026)
- Why: on 23 Nov btrfs ran out of unallocated space. `df` still showed 116 GiB free, but metadata couldn't grow, so every commit failed with "No space left on device". Later, five full suites at once took disk I/O pressure to 50%. Claude caught both by hand every half hour. This makes it udl's.
- Build: `orch/health.py`.
  - **`HostHealth`,** per host:
    - `memory_available`, `swap_used`, `swap_total`;
    - `io_pressure` (`/proc/pressure/io`'s `some avg60`, %);
    - `disks: list[DiskHealth]`, for every path udl uses on the host (its `work_dir` and each repo's checkout there): `free` and `size` (`df -B1`), and on btrfs (`stat -f -c %T` says `btrfs`) also `unallocated`, `metadata_used` and `metadata_total` (`btrfs filesystem usage -b`, which needs no root for these);
    - `harnesses`: each harness service's `MemoryCurrent`/`MemoryMax` (`systemctl --user show`);
    - `gates`: running and allowed;
    - `at`.
  - **Reading it:** one `sh -c` script through `HostShell`, printing a JSON line per measure, read by a typed parser. A measure a host can't give (no PSI, not Linux, not btrfs) is `None` and shown as "not measured", never as zero.
  - **Thresholds** (`[apps.orch.health]`): `memory_min = "2GiB"`, `swap_max_ratio = 0.9`, `disk_min = "10GiB"`, `btrfs_unallocated_min = "1GiB"`, `btrfs_metadata_max_ratio = 0.9`, `io_pressure_max = 40` (the 23 Nov incident ran at 45–70%), `check_minutes = 10`.
  - **Issues:** each breach is a `HealthIssue` (`host`, `kind`, `detail`, `hint`), and the hint names the fix. For example:
    - btrfs unallocated: "a data balance frees it: `sudo btrfs balance start -dusage=10 <mount>`, which needs root, so a person runs it";
    - disk: "udl removes a task's worktree after its merge; look for others".
  - **What it does is a setting** (the user, 23 Nov 2026: "Allow the user to set what they want it to do out of these options, with act + tell as default"): `on_breach: Literal["act_and_tell", "tell", "act"] = "act_and_tell"`.
    - **act:** while a host has an open issue, the dispatcher starts no new turn there and the gate lane starts no new gate there. What's running finishes; a gate over its own cap is 27.12's. A check that finds the host healthy releases it.
    - **tell:** one `HumanAction` (28.2, "To action", `when = now`) per open issue (host + kind), with the detail and hint. It isn't raised twice while the issue stays open, and udl closes it when the issue clears (its result: "cleared at …").
    - **Either way:** `orch.host.unhealthy` and `orch.host.healthy` events carry the issues.
  - **When it runs:** every `check_minutes` from the dispatcher's loop, and before a gate starts when the last check is older than that.
  - **Operations:** `orch_host_health` (read-only; `host?`) → `list[HostHealth]` with their issues. CLI `udl orch hosts health`; MCP and API as every operation.
  - **The inbox:** its agents strip (28.4) gets a line for a host that is held.
- Tests first:
  - the parser over captured outputs: ext4, btrfs, no PSI, macOS;
  - each threshold becomes its issue;
  - `act` holds turns and gates and releases them on recovery;
  - `tell` raises one action, never twice, and closes it when the issue clears;
  - `act_and_tell` does both;
  - the events.
- Done when:
  - on the fakes, a host whose btrfs unallocated space drops under its threshold holds new gates, raises a To action that names the balance, and resumes when it recovers;
  - `udl orch hosts health` reads real numbers on jeff-wiggle. Claude runs it.

**27.14 Usage left, per agent** · needs 25.3, 27.7, 27.10, 28.2 · size M (the user, 23 Nov 2026: "how much usage agents have left (including free, using requests sent) … retrieved via mcp/cli and also displayed via the web interface")
- Build: `orch/usage.py`.
  - **`orch_usage`** (read-only; `agent?`) → `list[AgentUsage]`, one per agent: its runner, model and provider, plus:
    - `paid: KeyUsage | None`: the provider key it spends from, read through the provider's `health()` (25.3: OpenRouter's `GET /key`). It holds the key's label, `spent`, `limit` and `left` (the provider's `limit_remaining`), and `spent_today` when the provider gives it. Never the key itself.
      - **The provider API grows for this:** `ProviderHealth` (25.2) gains `key: KeyStatus | None` (`label`, `spent`, `limit`, `left`, `spent_today`, `free_tier: bool | None`), beside `credit_left`. OpenRouter's `_KeyInfo` parses `usage`, `limit`, `limit_remaining`, `usage_daily` and `is_free_tier` from `GET /key` and fills it in, `FakeOpenRouter` serves those fields, and the provider contract suite checks a provider that says nothing leaves `key` as `None`.
    - `free: FreeUsage | None`, for a model on a free tier:
      - `requests_today`: udl's own count of the model requests the agent's sessions made since the allowance's last reset. Each runner `Usage` event in the transcript (27.7) is one request.
      - `allowance`, `left`, `resets_at`, and `source = "counted"`, since the provider doesn't say what's left of a free tier.
    - `own`: the agent's own turns, tokens and cost today and in all, summed over its sessions (27.7, 27.10).
    - `limited_until`, from its session;
    - `state: "ok" | "low" | "out"`: `low` once `left` is under `usage_low_ratio` (0.1) of the limit or allowance, `out` at nothing or while limited.
  - **Allowances** (`[apps.orch.allowances]`), keyed by `provider:model-glob`. The default, for OpenRouter's `*:free` models, is OpenRouter's rule: 50 requests a day, or 1,000 once the account has bought $10 of credits (the key's `is_free_tier` is false), resetting at 00:00 UTC (11:00 AEDT, as the ERNIE agents saw). An allowance is the account's, so agents on one account share its `left`, and each has its own `requests_today`.
  - **CLI** `udl orch agents usage` (a table: agent, model, left, today, resets, state); MCP and API as every operation. The `orch.usage.low` and `orch.usage.out` events fire when an agent crosses into either state.
  - **A paid key going `low`** raises a To action, "raise the cap?" (28.2), once per key per cap.
  - **`AgentInfo`** (27.9) and the inbox's `AgentLine` (28.4) gain `usage`: one line, such as `$3.36 left of $8` or `412 of 1,000 requests left, resets 11:00`. 29.6 draws it.
- Tests first:
  - `ProviderHealth.key` filled from a `FakeOpenRouter` key (`usage`, `limit`, `limit_remaining`, `usage_daily`, `is_free_tier`), and a paid key's left read from it;
  - a free model's requests counted from transcript `Usage` events since the last 00:00 UTC, and two agents on one account sharing its allowance;
  - 50 vs 1,000 by `is_free_tier`;
  - `low` and `out` crossings raise their events once;
  - a low paid key raises one To action;
  - the key never appears in a result, a log or an event.
- Done when: on the fakes, `udl orch agents usage` shows a paid agent's money left and a free agent's requests left, with its reset time; and the same answer comes over MCP.

**Phase 27 is done when:** `udl orch agents set fake-a --runner fake …`, `udl orch assign 22.2 fake-a`, and the task reaches `ready` with its PR linked, on the fakes, with `udl serve` restarted once in the middle.

---

## Phase 28: Human comms: Q&A, Ready to merge, To action

This is human-comms.md as data. Its three sections become three lists with the same rules: every item says what it is about and links to it, an answer or result is acted on and then moved to Done with what was done about it.

### Tests first
- A question's answer is delivered into the asking agent's session, unblocks its task, and is written as a `decision` note on every task and feature the question links to.
- Ready to merge orders a stack so that a PR never comes before one it says "Merge after"; a merge moves the task, removes the worktree and sends each PR stacked on it back to its agent to rebase.
- The inbox is the same from the CLI, the API and MCP, by one snapshot test of its model.

### Tasks

**28.1 Questions** · needs 27.7
- Build: `Question`: `key` (`Q-<n>`), `project`, `asked_by`, `to` (an agent's name or `humans`), `title`, `text` (markdown), `options: list[str]`, `blocking: bool`, `state: open | answered | closed`, `answer`, `answered_by`, `outcome` (what was done about the answer). `Message` records hold the thread (follow-ups both ways). Links name the tasks and features it is about.
  - `orch_ask` (`task`, `title`, `text`, `options?`, `blocking`): from an agent. It goes to the agent's manager where that manager has `answer`, else to `humans`. Blocking moves the task to `blocked` and the agent on to its next queued task; non-blocking leaves the agent working.
  - `orch_block` (`task`, `reason`) is `orch_ask` with `blocking` set, for "only a person can do this".
  - `orch_answer` (`question`, `answer`): records it; writes a `decision` note ("Q-7, answered by …: …") on each linked task and feature; moves a blocked task back to `queued` at the front; and the dispatcher delivers the answer into the agent's session.
  - `orch_reply` (a follow-up from either side), `orch_escalate` (a manager passes it up), `orch_close_question` (`outcome`).
  - `orch_list_questions` (`state`, `to`, `task`), `orch_get_question`.
- Tests first: `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_questions.py`: the phase's first bullet; routing with and without a manager.
- Done when: no question can be answered without its answer landing on the tasks it names.

- **Built (`deepseek-b`, 21 Nov 2026, PR #63):** `orch/questions.py` — `QuestionRecords` (read by project, key, state, who it is asked of and the task it was asked in; `save_question`, `question`, `questions`, `thread`, `say`), `ask`, `block`, `answer`, `reply`, `escalate`, `close`, `route` and `named`. The records are `orch_question` (`Question`, unique on `(project, key)`, its `Q-` number from 26.3's `Counters`) and `orch_message` (`Message`, the thread, by the question's id), and deleting a project takes its questions, threads, notes and links with it (`records._delete_questions`). **What a question is about is its links** (26.4): the task it was asked in and any other task or feature it names, `udl:<key>`, so an answer has somewhere to land and `orch_get_question` reads them back. **Routing** (`route`, `_up_from`): the asker's manager when that agent is a `manager` with `manages.answer`, up the chain to the first that may, and `humans` at the top — a manager that may not answer is not told. **Blocking** is one write with the question (the question, its first message, its links, the task to `blocked` with the question's key on it, and the move's note): the agent's loop then takes its next queued task (27.7). **Answering** is one write too: the question `answered`, its answer in the thread, a `decision` note on every task and feature it names — `Q-1, answered by <who>: <answer>` — and the task, when it was blocked on that question, `queued` again at the front of its agent's queue (the rest renumbered). `escalate` moves `to` up a step and marks `escalated`; `close` records `outcome` and leaves a blocked task blocked (closing is not answering); `reply` is either side's follow-up. **Who may answer:** an agent only a question asked of it (the operation refuses another's); the role table gives an implementer `orch_ask`/`orch_block`/`orch_reply` and the read-only two, and a manager those plus `orch_answer`/`orch_escalate`/`orch_close_question`. **27.7's deferred questions are wired here:** a stall, a failed session and a pull only waiting on the bot each ask (blocking for the first two, not for the bot's, and once per task), instead of putting a line of text on `blocked_on`; and the answer reaches the session — a session that picks the task up again is told what was answered while it waited, fenced as udl's own words. Tests, written first: 27 in `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_questions.py` (routing with and without a manager; a blocking question blocks and a non-blocking one does not; **the done-when** — the answer written on two tasks and a feature as `decision` notes; the task back at the front; the answer and its notes one write over a store that cannot write notes; a second answer refused; an unknown key; `in_progress` blocked; the thread both ways; escalating up to a manager's manager and to the humans; closing with its outcome and leaving the task blocked; the listing narrowed by state and who; one question with its thread; an unknown task; the kinds), 9 in `test_dispatcher.py` (a stall asked of the agent's manager, the bot's wait asked once, the answer in the next session's first prompt, and the review's: a stall that blocks whatever question is open, a blocking ask that fails being raised, an answer told once, another's answer not told, a non-blocking answer reaching a running session, and the bot's wait not asked again once answered), and the two 27.7 tests updated to read the question where they read a line of text. `openapi.json` and the typed client regenerated.

**28.2 Human actions** · needs 26.3
- Build: `HumanAction`: `key` (`A-<n>`), `project`, `title`, `text`, `when: now | after | later`, `after: str | None` (a task key: it becomes `now` when that task merges), `assignee` (`human:<name>`), `state: open | done | dropped`, `result`. A task of kind `human` creates one when it is assigned.
  - Operations: `orch_set_action`, `orch_list_actions`, `orch_finish_action` (`key`, `result`: writes the result as a note on the linked task and, for a `human` task, moves it to `merged`), `orch_drop_action`.
- Tests first: `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_actions.py`: `after` turning into `now`.
- Done when: human-comms.md's "To action" section can be entered item for item.
- **Built (`deepseek-b`, 20 Nov 2026, PR #48):** `orch/actions.py` — `ActionRecords` (read by project, state, when, person and task; `save_action`, `action`, `actions`), `set_action`, `become_now`, `finish`, `drop`, `for_task` and `_action`. The record is `orch_action` (`HumanAction`, unique on `(project, key)`, its `A-` number from 26.3's `Counters`), and deleting a task, a feature or a project deletes its actions with it, in one place (`records._delete_actions`). The shape is `orch.models.Action`, with `ActionWhen` (`now | after | later`) and `ActionState` (`open | done | dropped`). The card's fields plus **`task`**: the task an action is about, so its result has somewhere to go and 26.4's links can hang on it. **`when` and `after` must agree** — an action `after` names a task and becomes `now` when that task merges (`become_now`, for 28.3 to call on a merge), and one that is `now` or `later` names none; an assignee that is not `human:<name>` is refused. **`finish`** writes the result as a `progress` note on the task it is about (author `human` — udl does not know which person, and the CLI's caller is the owner), and for a `human` task it moves the task to `merged` (a person may move a task anywhere; the table's rows are for agents), clears its assignee and closes up the person's queue behind it. **A person's task gets its action when it is assigned** (`plan_for_task` from `orch.queue.assign`, written in the queue's own batch: one write, under its locks — round 2 below), and assigning it to another person moves the action with it: a task has one action, not one per assignee. Operations: `orch_set_action`, `orch_list_actions` (read-only; `state` None means the open ones, the "To action" list), `orch_finish_action`, `orch_drop_action` (idempotent). Tests, written first: `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_actions.py` (17: the key and its next, the due order, `after` without a task and `after` on a `now` action refused, an agent as assignee refused, **`after` turning into `now`** and only the open ones, finishing (its result, a second finish refused), the note on the task, a person's task merging and leaving the list, dropping with its reason, an action that is not there, a person's task getting an action and keeping one when reassigned, an agent's task getting none, and the record in `Orch.record_kinds()`), `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_actions_operations.py` (7: each operation through `service.call()`, the narrowed listing, the requests and answers). `openapi.json` and the typed client regenerated.
- **Review round 1 (`deepseek-b`, 20 Nov 2026):** the bot's six, each with its test. (1) **Deleting takes the actions with it**: `records._delete_actions` is called from `delete_task`, `delete_feature` (its tasks') and `delete_project` (every action of the project, so its `A-` numbers start over rather than colliding on `A-1`). (2) `finish` on a person's task **makes what waited `after` it `now`** (`become_now`, since a person's task merges with no PR for 28.3 to see), and **refuses before writing anything** when that task is already `merged` — a second action about it would otherwise write its note and then fail the move. (3) `for_task` **reopens a dropped action** instead of handing it to the next person. (4) `drop` **answers an already-dropped action unchanged**, so the operation's `idempotent` hint is true. (5) `set_action` **refuses a `task` or `after` that is not a task of the project** (`DoesNotExist`), and `human:` with no name says so instead of calling it an agent's name. (6) `unassign` **takes the action off the person** (`assignee=None`, still open) so a task nobody holds is not on someone's To action.
- **Review round 2 (`deepseek-b`, 20 Nov 2026, #48):** the To action is **one write with the queue**, not two: `actions.plan_for_task` (and `plan_action` under it) answers the action with its `A-` number already claimed, and `queue._assign` / `queue._unassign` carry it in `save_all(..., also=[...])` inside the queue's own lock, so a store that cannot write the action writes neither (two tests over a store that refuses an action's write: `test_a_person_s_task_and_its_action_are_one_write`, `test_unassigning_a_person_s_task_writes_the_action_off_them_with_it`). The `A-` number's claim stays its own compare-and-set — a batch cannot take it — so a failed batch burns a number and writes no action. **The To-action section of `planning/human-comms.md` is 28.4's inbox**: what is built here is the record and its operations, not the section that shows it.

**28.3 Ready to merge** · needs 27.6, 25.5
- Build: `merge.py`.
  - `orch_merge_list` (read-only) → `MergeList`: `ready` (tasks in `ready`, grouped by repo, in merge order: a topological sort of "Merge after" within each stack, then by when they became ready), `coming` (tasks with an open PR that isn't ready, each with its blockers), `held` (with the reason), `done` (merged in the last 14 days, with what followed). Every row has the PR's URL, title, repo in full, head SHA, and the task and feature keys.
  - `orch_merge` (`task`, `confirm` = `owner/repo#number`; admin, owner-only unless the project's `merge = agent` and the caller is the assignee): re-checks readiness for the current head, then `github_merge_pull` with that head's SHA (so a PR that changed since isn't merged blind).
  - After a merge, however it happened (the watcher's `github.pr.merged`): the task moves to `merged` (and to `done` at once if a reviewer or the owner merged and the project has no promotion branches); its worktree is removed; each task stacked on it gets a `system` note and its agent a prompt to rebase onto the base and push again; actions waiting `after` it become `now`.
  - A PR closed without merging moves its task back to `in_progress` and tells its agent why (the closing comment, as data).
  - Promotion, for projects that have branches after the base: `orch_promotion` (`project`) lists what is `merged` and not `done` per repo; `orch_promote` (`tasks`, `note`; reviewer or owner) marks them `done` after the promotion PR merges. udl's own project has none.
- Tests first: `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_merge.py`: the phase's second bullet; `confirm` checked; a head that moved between listing and merging is refused.
- Done when: a three-PR stack merges in order on the fakes, each rebase prompted.
- **Built (`deepseek-b`, 20 Nov 2026, PR #56):** `orch/merge.py` (`after_merge`, `after_close`, `stacked`, `order`, `DAYS`) and `orch_merge_list`, `orch_merge`, `orch_promotion`, `orch_promote` in `plugin.py`. A merge is set off by one function, whether udl made it (`orch_merge`) or the watcher saw it (`github.pr.merged` → `pull_happened`, through `follow`'s new `happened` callback, so `follow` keeps its narrow `PullReader`): the task moves to `merged` — and to `done` at once when the merge was a review and nothing follows the base — the tasks stacked on it (`needs`) get a `system` note to rebase and push again (a note, not a prompt: nudging the agent's session is 27.7's), and actions waiting `after` it are `now` (28.2's `become_now`). `after_merge` is idempotent: a task already `merged`/`done` gets no second note, so udl's own merge and the watcher's event for it write once. A pull closed without merging moves its task back to `in_progress` with the pull's last comment as a `system` note. `orch_merge` (`key`, `confirm` = `owner/repo#number`, `sha` = the head the caller saw, which is required) re-assesses at that head and refuses a pull that moved since it was listed, rather than merging blind; `github_merge_pull` is called as udl (it is no agent's operation), and an agent may merge only where the project's `merge = agent` and the task is its own (`orch_merge` is in the implementer's role set for exactly that, and it stays an MCP tool so that path works there too; anyone else is refused by the operation). `merge_list` reads every pull at once and a pull udl cannot read is that row's blocker, not the list's failure; `done` is the last 14 days. **27.6's deferred branch:** where the project's `merge = agent` and the caller is the task's own assignee, `orch_ready` merges through `_merge_now` — the same call `orch_merge` makes — instead of moving the task to `ready`. **Promotion:** `orch_promotion` lists what is `merged` and not `done`; `orch_promote` (`tasks`, `note`) refuses a project with no `promotion` branches and is all-or-nothing (every key checked before one is written), then marks them `done` in one batch with a `progress` note each. **Tests, written first:** 31 in `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_merge_operations.py` (the hints and shapes; the list's four groups; a pull udl cannot read as its own blocker; the linked head; a stack of three in merge order; the 14 days; merging at its head; a moved head refused and the listed head merged; the wrong `confirm`; not-ready; a person's merge done at once; an agent refused, and allowed where the project says so; `ready`'s merge branch, its other side, and that it really merges; the rebase notes; the action becoming `now`; no pull; promote all-or-nothing and with no promotion branches; and the card's done-when — three pulls merged in order, each prompting the next), 10 in `test_merge_after.py` (the state a merge leaves, the note, who merged, the stack, `stacked`, the action, a merge seen twice writing once, a closed pull, one nobody started), 7 in `test_merge_watcher.py` (both event paths, the review bot's merge, a person's merge, an already-merged task, a pull no task has, and the loop's callback), and 7 in `test_merge.py` for `order()` alone. `openapi.json` and the typed client regenerated. **Not done here, and why:** the merged task's worktree is taken away by 27.5's `remove()` (`workspace.py`), which is READY in #52 but not in `origin/main` when this branched, so this PR leaves the task's `worktree` field as it is; wiring it is the two lines `_after_merge` used to hold, once #52 is in. The tests are unit-level rather than the integration file the card names: the fake `github` app inside a real `UDLService` covers the same path (the pull read, the merge, the notes) and the integration harness would repeat it.

**28.4 The inbox** · needs 28.1–28.3
- Build: `orch_inbox` (read-only; `project?`, `for = humans`) → `Inbox`: `merge: MergeList`, `questions` (open, blocking first, newest first), `actions` (`now`, then `after`, then `later`), `agents` (each agent's status in a line), `updated_at`. `udl orch inbox` renders it as human-comms.md reads today: three headed sections, each item with its links. `--watch` redraws on `orch.*` events.
- Tests first: `tests/interface/test_orch_inbox.py`: the phase's third bullet.
- Done when: Claude, as an MCP client, gets from one call what it reads from human-comms.md today.
- **Built (deepseek-a, 22 Nov 2026, PR #76):** `orch_inbox` (`InboxRequest`: `project?` → `Inbox`: `project`, `merge: MergeList`, `questions`, `actions`, `agents`, `updated_at`), read-only and `open_world` (it reads the pulls' state on GitHub, as 28.3's list does), with `udl orch inbox`. The sections are the app's own: 28.3's `merge_list` (ready, coming, held, done — its order and its blockers), 28.1's open questions sorted blocking-first then newest-first, 28.2's actions in its own order, and an `AgentLine` per agent (`name`, `title`, `role`, `state`, `task`, `since`, and `line`: `deepseek-a — implementing 28.4`). The question sort needed the records' `created_at`, which the `Question` shape does not carry, so `inbox` reads `QuestionRecords` itself rather than going through `list_questions`. `AgentLine` is derived from `27.1`'s agent records and `27.7`'s sessions (`agents.Agent.status`), never stored in the database; `AgentRole` and `AgentStatus` moved from `agents.py` to `models.py` (where the shapes are) and `agents.py` re-exports them, so a shape can name them without the cycle, and `as_shape`'s bound is `BaseModel` (the agents' own `_Model` is not models'). **`--watch`:** a CLI-only flag, so it is not a request field and the API and MCP never see it — `cli_command` takes a `watcher` (`cli_groups` and `add_commands` take `watchers`, by operation name) and adds `--watch` to a command that has one, running it instead of showing the answer once. The CLI's own map wires it (`app.PLUGIN_WATCHERS`), so nothing in the app imports the CLI. `render.watch_inbox` draws the inbox, then looks every `WATCH_POLL` (2 s) at the newest `orch.*` event **stored** — not at this process's bus: the dispatcher, the GitHub watcher and the API's asks publish in `udl serve`'s process, so a watch listening to its own bus would draw once and wait forever — and draws again when that is a new one; a burst is one draw. It also draws again when `WATCH_REFRESH` (30 s) is up with nothing published, so a change that publishes no event (an ask over the API, a task moved by hand) still shows, and it says why and keeps going when the inbox cannot be read (GitHub down). Until 28.5's event types exist, the refresh is what makes it live; the event path is tested with a stored event. **Rendering:** `render.inbox` prints the three sections as human-comms.md reads (`Ready to merge` with each pull's URL and blocker, `held`, `Coming` and `Merged lately` beneath it, `Questions` with `blocking` marked and who asked, `To action` with `now`/`after`/`later`, `Agents` with each one's line), each section saying when it is empty. `AgentLine.line` is written by the app (`models.agent_line`) rather than computed: a `@computed_field` is left out of pydantic's validation schema, and the MCP tool's structured output checks its own answer against that, so a computed line was refused. The questions are the ones asked of the humans (`to = humans`): a question a manager is asked waits on that agent. **Tests, written first:** `tests/interface/test_orch_inbox.py` (the card's own: one snapshot, `tests/interface/snapshots/orch_inbox.json`, answered the same by the CLI, the API and MCP — two tests, since a CLI run cannot start inside a running loop; the sections' contents; the agent line; a question asked of a manager left out; the watch showing what a second service over the same database changed; the hints), 4 in `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_inbox_operations.py` over a real service and a fake `github` (the three lists, the blocking ones first and each group newest first, another project's things left out, the request's one field), 9 in `tests/unit/interfaces/cli/test_render.py` (the three sections and their lines, the empty ones, the watch drawing once then once per new stored event, the refresh timer drawing with nothing published, and four for an outage: saying why and carrying on when the inbox cannot be read, saying it again when it outlasts a draw, a second outage said too, and one outage said once while it draws anyway), and 2 in `tests/unit/interfaces/cli/test_adapters.py` (a command that follows takes `--watch` and runs its watcher; one that does not has no such option). `orch_inbox` was added to `tests/interface/test_orch_operations.py`'s catalogue, so its command, route and tool are checked with the rest. `openapi.json` and the typed client regenerated.

**28.5 Events** · needs 28.4
- Build: event types with schemas and examples: `orch.task.changed` (`from`, `to`), `orch.task.ready`, `orch.task.blocked`, `orch.task.merged`, `orch.question.asked`, `orch.question.answered`, `orch.action.needed`, `orch.agent.limited`, `orch.agent.idle` (an empty queue), `orch.session.started` / `ended`, `orch.link.changed`. A README example: a `notify` subscription on `orch.question.asked where blocking == true`.
- Tests first: each type is registered and each is published once at its moment (asserted in the integration tests above).
- Done when: `udl events types` lists them with schemas.
- **Built (deepseek-b, 22 Nov 2026, PR #79):** the seven types the phase was missing — `orch.task.changed` (`from`, `to`, `by`), `orch.task.blocked` (`on`), `orch.task.merged`, `orch.question.asked` (`question`, `task`, `blocking`, `asked_by`, `to`), `orch.question.answered`, `orch.action.needed` (`action`, `when`, `assignee`) and `orch.agent.idle` — in `orch/events.py`, each with a schema and a worked example, all of them declared by `Orch.event_types()` so `udl events types` lists them with their fields. The rest the phase already had (`orch.task.ready`, `orch.session.*`, `orch.agent.limited`, `orch.link.changed`, and `orch.task.merged`, which 28.7 publishes where the merge happens, with the pull and the sha) are unchanged.
  **Where each is published, and why there:** `orch.task.changed` (and `blocked`/`merged` when that is what the move was) from `TaskRecords.save_all()` — the one write every move goes through, in the same batch as its `StateChange` history, keyed by that history's id — so no path (the queue, the dispatcher, the merge, a person's move) can forget it and none publishes it twice; a save that leaves the state alone says nothing. `AppRecords` gained an optional `events` (the framework gives every app's records its `AppEvents`), which is how the records layer publishes at all; an event that fails to publish is logged and never undoes the write. Questions publish from `orch.questions` (asked, and answered), actions from `ActionRecords.save_action()` and `say_needed()` — the queue calls it for the action it writes in its own batch, so a person's task is never queued without its event — and `orch.agent.idle` from the dispatcher's `_step`, once per idle stretch (a loop that ticks every 30 s would otherwise say it every tick), again when it runs out of work again.
  **The README** has the card's example: a `notify` action and a subscription `--on orch.question.asked --where 'blocking == true'`, beside a line on each of the new types.
  Tests, written first: `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_events.py` (25: every card type declared and listed by `list_event_types` with its fields and example, every type's example valid and its subject non-empty, `from`/`to` in the changed type's schema, a move published once and a repeat save silent, each move its own event, blocked naming what it waits on, a move to `merged` published as a move, a question asked and answered, an action needed, and a person's task's action) and `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_events.py` (3: an idle agent says so once across four ticks, a restarted udl's idle is news again, and `list_events` reads it as any other). `AppEvents.emit` and `EventType.validate` now dump by alias, so a field named like a keyword is `from` in the event's data and in a filter.   Nothing in the OpenAPI spec changed (`make openapi --check` is clean).
  **Review round 1 (PR #79):** four items. (1) The moves `orch.questions` writes itself — a blocking question's block, its answer's unblocking, and `block_with` — went through the raw `AppRecords.save_all`, so they published nothing and skipped `_follow_all`. They now go through `TaskRecords.save_all(..., clock=...)` with the question in `also`: still one write, and the move is published and in the history as any other move is (the `StateChange` is the save's, as the queue's is). (2) `orch.agent.idle`'s key was the process's own counter, and `(type, dedupe_key)` is unique for good — so a restarted udl's first stretches were dropped as seen. The key now carries the dispatcher run's own id, `agent.idle:<agent>:<run>:<n>`. (3) The queue's `say_needed` path for a person's task had no test of its own; it has one now (a human task assigned publishes `orch.action.needed` with its `task` and `assignee`), so the count below is true. (4) A nit: `EventTypeList` is imported at the module's top, not inside the test. (5) Rebased onto main's 28.7, one thing had to be fixed: the merger's stack test hung, because a stop that lands while a loop rests between turns could be swallowed by the cleanup of its tick and its wake, leaving the loop resting again on a clock nobody moves. `_rest` now gathers the two waits and re-raises when its own task is being cancelled; the merger's own test is what caught it, and `test_a_stop_that_lands_while_the_loop_rests_ends_it` guards the path.
  **Review round 2 (PR #79):** one nit — the test file's two imports of the records are one line. Rebased onto main (`1fec3d9`, 29.2's web skeleton), with main's `test_progress_operations.py` taken as it is there.

**28.6 End to end** · needs 28.5
- **Built (deepseek-b, 23 Nov 2026, PR #90):** `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_end_to_end.py`, one orchestration through a real `udl serve`, driven only by the CLI and the agent's own MCP calls. The agent is `tests/support/orch_e2e_plugins/orch_scripted.py`, a local plugin (the test's `plugin_dirs`): a runner whose turns are a script in `turns.json`, each turn a list of steps (`mcp`: a call on udl's MCP server with the session's own token; `shell`: a program in the session's folder; `wait`), chosen by what the prompt says (`when`), so a turn the dispatcher adds on its own — a nudge, a restart, the pull's news — uses no turn written for later. GitHub is `FakeGitHub` served over HTTP, the repo is a temp git repo with a bare origin and a gate that passes in no time. Every git the test runs of its own goes through `tests/support/git.py`'s `git()`, which drops the repo variables a pre-commit hook exports: with them, a raw `git init --bare` under `make check` re-initialises the checkout the hook was run from (it did, once).
  **The flow, as the card has it:** a person sets up the account, host, project, repo, agent, action and subscription and imports the plan; the agent asks a blocking question (`orch_ask`), the task goes `blocked`, and a person answers it on the CLI; the answer reaches the next session's prompt; the agent writes, commits and pushes (`orch_push`, the real `git push` and `git pull`), the fake bot's first comment has one item; the agent's next turn fixes it, commits and pushes again, so the pull is on a new head, and the bot's verification of that head is clean; the agent calls `orch_ready`; Ready to merge lists it with the sha it is ready at (the new head), and a person merges it (`orch merge --key 1.1 --confirm yelrom0/udl#1 --sha <head>`); the task is `done` — the card's "merged", but 28.3's rule makes a person's merge of a project with nothing after its base `done` at once — its worktree and branch are gone (the directory and the branch checked, not only the record), and the answer is on its notes.
  **Two things the run needed that the plan did not have, both fixed here:** (1) a merged task kept its worktree — `space.remove()` existed and nothing called it — so `_merge_now` now takes the worktree and the branch away after `_after_merge` (`_removed()`), writing on the task's notes and keeping the merge when that fails, and clearing `worktree`/`branch` on the record; (2) `merge-list`'s row carried no sha, because a pull link's state is only refreshed by a `github.pr.*` event, and no watcher runs without an enabled subscription — so the e2e subscribes one (`github.pr.*` → a `notify` action), which is what makes udl's github watcher look at the repository at all; with `[integrations.github] poll_every = "1s"` the link is current within a tick.
  **What `_removed()` may not do:** it never fails a merge (the pull is merged whatever became of the checkout), it leaves the worktree of the agent that made the merge itself — with `merge = agent` that agent's session is in the folder — until that session ends, and it leaves one whose agent udl no longer knows rather than guessing this host, saying so on the task's notes either way. Its four cases are tested in `test_merge_operations.py`: taken away, a repo with no checkout (the note, the merge kept, `orch.task.merged` still published), an agent udl does not know, and an agent's own merge — that one driven through `orch_ready`, which is how a `merge = agent` project actually merges.
  **Left out:** a self-merged task's worktree stays until a person takes it away (`git worktree remove` and `git branch -D` in the repo's checkout). Nothing in udl removes it when that session ends: the dispatcher's session-end path would have to, and 28.8's checkout does not (it only fast-forwards `Repo.checkouts`). The task's notes say so, and the follow-up is a session-end hook that calls the same removal.
  Tests, written first: the e2e itself (the card's own), which failed at each step above until each was fixed. It passes three times running with `-p no:randomly` and once with the default random order, and again with a hook's `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_INDEX_FILE` exported; `test_merge_operations.py` is green beside it (36 tests together).
- Build: `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_end_to_end.py`, through a real `udl serve` on `FakeRunner`, `FakeGitHub` and a temp git repo, driven only by CLI commands and the fake agent's MCP calls: import a plan → assign a task → the agent asks a blocking question → answered from the CLI → the agent pushes, the fake bot finds one item, the agent fixes it, the bot's verification is clean → Ready to merge lists it → merged → the task is `merged`, the worktree gone, the decision on the task's notes.
- Done when: it passes three times running with `pytest -p no:randomly` and with random ordering.

**28.7 Auto merge** · needs 28.3, 27.8 · size L (O-D13, O-C13)
- Build:
  - **The setting.** `Project` gains `auto_merge: bool = False` and `merger: str | None` (an agent's name).
    - Saving checks that the merger is an agent with role `reviewer` or `manager`, whose `projects` include this one (or are all).
    - Turning auto merge on needs a merger.
    - `merge = human | agent` is unchanged: auto merge sits beside it, and wins while it is on.
  - **`orch_set_auto_merge`** (`project`, `on`, `merger?`), owner only. An agent's or a chat's token is refused, so no model can give itself the merge. It publishes `orch.project.changed`. CLI: `udl orch projects auto-merge udl --on --merger claude`.
  - **It is the only writer of `auto_merge` and `merger`.** `orch_set_project`, the importer and every other write keep both as stored: a change to either through them is refused (`NotAllowed`, naming `orch_set_auto_merge`). Otherwise a chat's owner-level token could turn auto merge on through `orch_set_project`.
  - **The merger's turn.**
    - The merger's standing session (27.8's; a reviewer gets one too) is prompted with each task of the project that becomes `ready`, in Ready to merge's order (28.3). The task comes as data: its spec, the PR's URL, the head SHA and the diff's stat.
    - It reviews the diff against the task's spec and the project's rules.
    - Then it calls `orch_merge` (`task`, `confirm`), or `orch_review_task` with `changes` and its reasons. That moves the task back to `in_progress`, and the reasons go to its agent as a note.
    - One PR at a time per project, and the queue always moves on:
      - after a merge, the next ready task is prompted;
      - after `changes`, the task has left `ready`, so the next ready task that isn't stacked on it is prompted at once (one stacked on it waits for it, as it would for a person);
      - the merger's own task (O-C13) is merged by it only when the project has a review bot. Without one, it is skipped: it stays under Ready now marked "waiting for a person: the merger's own task", and the next one not stacked on it is prompted.
    - A merger may call `orch_review_task` on any of its project's `ready` tasks, outside its own subtree too, so a `manager` merger isn't refused the review it needs.
  - **`orch_merge` for the merger** (it widens 28.3's rule). The merger may merge when:
    - it is the project's merger and auto merge is on;
    - the task is `ready`;
    - it is not the task's assignee, or the project has a review bot (O-C13).

    Readiness is re-checked for the current head, as it is for anyone. The merge's note and `orch.task.merged` carry `by` and `auto: true`.
  - **The lists:** `orch_merge_list` and `orch_inbox` carry `auto_merge` and `merger`, and each ready row carries what the merger is doing with it (`reviewing`, `next`, `changes asked`).
  - **udl's own project** (30.3) turns it on, with `claude` as its merger. That is the user's standing rule since 17 Nov 2026: Claude merges a PR once CI is green and the review bot has nothing open.
- Tests first: `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_auto_merge.py`:
  - with auto merge on, the merger merges two stacked PRs in order, with nobody else acting; with it off, nothing is merged;
  - the merger can't merge another project's task, or one whose head moved;
  - the merger's own task: merged by it with a review bot set; without one, skipped and marked, with the next one merged;
  - a `changes` verdict moves on to the next ready task that isn't stacked on it, and a `manager` merger can review a task outside its subtree;
  - `orch_set_project` can't change `auto_merge` or `merger`;
  - an implementer's token is refused `orch_merge` while auto merge is on;
  - `orch_set_auto_merge` is refused to an agent's token and to a chat's;
  - a merger that isn't a reviewer or a manager can't be saved.
- Done when: on the fakes, a reviewer merger takes a stack of three PRs from Ready to merge to merged by itself.
- **Built (deepseek-a, 22 Nov 2026, PR #78):** `Project.auto_merge` and `merger`; `orch_set_auto_merge` (`on`, `merger?`, `project?`), the owner's alone (`admin`, no MCP tool: an agent's token is refused, and `PrincipalKind` has only `owner` and `agent`, so a chat's is an agent's, refused by the same check), which is the only writer of the two fields (`orch_set_project` refuses a change to either — on a new project too — naming it), and publishes `orch.project.changed` with the time in its dedupe key, so on-off-on is said every time. `udl orch projects auto-merge udl --on --merger claude` runs it. **The merger's turn:** the dispatcher gives a project's merger a turn of its own — a standing `merge` task in the project it merges (`merging` feature, never finished, with a worktree to read the diff in) and a prompt with **one** ready pull (the first it may act on): its task, title, URL, head SHA, feature, spec, the diff's `git` command in that worktree, and the exact `orch_review_task` and `orch_merge` calls. Its own ready pull is skipped where the project has no review bot, and a row with a blocker of its own — a check still running, or one stacked on a pull not merged — is nobody's to be on yet: neither is prompted, the list says why ("waiting for a person: the merger's own task", "it waits on F-1.1, which is ready"), and the one it is told of is the next that is free. The same list is not told twice (`Agent.last_merge_news`, its own field: a manager's quiet tick no longer wipes it). `orch_merge` widens 28.3: it is **not** admin (a merger may be a reviewer, and the role table cannot know which agent that is), so the operation's own checks decide — a person, the assignee where `merge = agent`, or the project's merger with auto merge on, a `ready` task, and not its own unless there is a review bot; it reads the pull as udl on its own behalf, and an auto merge is a review, so a project with nothing after its base sees the task `done`. A merge publishes `orch.task.merged` with `by` and `auto`, and its note says "Merged by claude (auto merge)". `orch_merge_list` and `orch_inbox` carry `auto_merge` and `merger`, and each ready row its `MergerState` (`reviewing`, `next`, `changes asked` — the merger's own verdict only, and only with auto merge on); only the merger's `changes` sends a ready task back to `in_progress`. **Not done:** the diff's stat is not on GitHub's pull shape (`get_pull` has no such field), so the prompt gives the `git diff` command in the merger's worktree instead. **Tests, written first:** 34 in `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_auto_merge.py` (the switch, its refusals — agent and owner — and its event; `set_project`'s refusals; the lists and their row states; the merger's merge, its refusals, the note and event, `done` and the promotion; the own-task marking), 8 in `tests/integration/plugins/apps/orch/test_dispatcher.py` (the turn merges a ready pull and is told the exact call; the same list once; its own task skipped; **a stack of three merged by itself, bottom first** — the done when; nothing merged with it off; a `changes` verdict moving on; its standing task in the project it merges, with a worktree; the next one it may act on, past its own and what is stacked on it), and 1 in `tests/interface/test_orch_operations.py` (the card's own CLI line, run for real). `orch_merge` re-checks `needs` at the head it merges (27.6's readiness), so a pull stacked on an unmerged one cannot be merged first, and that has its own test in `tests/unit/plugins/apps/orch/test_merge_operations.py`.

**28.8 The checkout follows `main`** · needs 28.3, 27.5 · size S (the user, 23 Nov 2026)
- Why: people read the plan in a repo's own checkout (`Repo.checkouts[host]`), while merges land on GitHub. On 23 Nov udl's checkout on jeff-wiggle was seven merges behind, so the user saw 27.9 as not started when it had merged.
- Build: after a merge, as part of 28.3's after-merge step, for each host in `Repo.checkouts`:
  - `git -C <checkout> fetch origin <base>`.
  - Then, if the checkout is on `<base>`, has nothing uncommitted, and can fast-forward: `git merge --ff-only origin/<base>`.
